## Still requires real-device and full-scope acceptance

User-TCC grant/deny/revoke/relaunch; real application capture; physical multi-monitor/mixed Retina/negative-origin/display-removal flows; complete capture → annotate → copy/export → pin → OCR → history paths; system/microphone audio sync and sustained recording; real camera selection/disconnect/indicator shutdown and overlay exclusion; Retina/user-desktop annotation effects; visible-preview force-kill, power loss and storage-removal recovery; long scrolling and prolonged resource use; broad multilingual/table/formula/inpainting/barcode quality; real Apple language download/translation; independent Office/export interoperability; sustained isolated-GIF resource behavior and every remaining parity-row requirement. Historical 0.6 introduced the narrow arcs/polylines, image-pin text selection and scroll-editing evidence above, with the final localized/composited window snapshots. App-identical Intel 0.6 passed its own final pipeline and both installed formats; physical external-app drag, direct Copy and >800 px scaled-preview equality remain unverified. ARM and Intel 0.7 presets/export/barcode work now pass their independent native and both-format installed gates; actual AX/real-device acceptance remains open. Click/scroll/keystroke effects, transparent/broader animation input, broader exceptional GIF cleanup and sustained codec resources remain open alongside all other Missing/Partial features; the specific outer-trim regression has source-qualified b44c1fb evidence. ARM/Intel 0.8 adds verified still WebP/AVIF and bounded recording WebP as qualified above; ARM/Intel b44c1fb Save/Retry and scoped GIF-stage changes now have independent native acceptance; broader real-device/exceptional cases and sustained resources remain open.

CI does not grant the user's OS permissions or modify permission databases. Packages are ad-hoc signed and **not notarized**; integrity checks do not establish publisher identity or Gatekeeper acceptance. Missing/Partial requirements remain in scope. No full-parity, zero-leak, universal latency or whole-device memory claim is made.

### Source scope and visible behavior

Accepted ARM154 persists a versioned editable document with original/base asset references, layer geometry/style, crop viewport, capture metadata, numbering and output decoration alongside a separately stored current raster. Save-to-History creates a new record; reopening restores the saved state, not the previous session's undo/redo chain. Crop retains the base and objects, and “取消裁剪（恢复完整底图）” works after reopening a saved viewport. Restoring the complete base cannot recreate pixels or layers lost before that base existed.

Local editable history retains original pixels and removable redaction layers. Reopening, removing those layers or uncropping can reveal sensitive content; this is not secure erasure. A regular flattened current-image export contains no editable layers. Explicit original copy/export reveals the stored source and must not be confused with the checked redacted current-image deliverable.

Editable pins reopen through Space/“标注”; Apply transactionally replaces current/document state while preserving the pin original. “临时隐藏标注（仅显示）” changes only the transient preview. Copy, Save and recognition use the saved annotated current image; original copy/save is explicit in “原始图片”. Hidden display can reveal sensitive pixels, and original/base/cropped-out local assets retain them. Historic rasters stay raster backgrounds; no old flattened layers are inferred. History's direct current-image Pin/Smart Erase and pin pixel transforms disclose flattening; they do not become editable-object-preserving routes.

Operation-aware pin admission accounts for retained rasters, redraw/hidden-preview work and draining output reservations. The configured 768 MiB managed-raster budgets and existing pin/disk/pixel limits are admission bounds, not process RSS. If both history commit and editor handoff fail for a supported captured image, one in-memory pending result blocks another capture until successful editor handoff/file commit or explicit discard. Its 512 MiB raster + 128 MiB encoded bound is not crash recovery; cancelled saves/dismissal preserve the pending result. Accepted ARM154 native/installed evidence covers the stated entry points and failure cases within its reported scope; physical input and sustained use remain unverified.

Owned-sRGB8 is selected by default only for eligible 8-bit sRGB drawing layouts. [DrawingRaster.unsupported](https://github.com/dandibbert/picshot/blob/2ca03e348255a9af6adb7341752bae8c0170d452/Sources/PicShot/DrawingRaster.swift#L205) requires the named RGB sRGB space, 8-bit nonfloating components and supported channel/alpha/byte-order/bitmap layouts; image masks and decode arrays are ineligible. Display P3, 16-bit, custom/linear/device profiles, decode arrays and other unsupported representations keep the original native path rather than being normalized to sRGB8 for display. Presentation/seed optimization does not replace original, base or model-source images; original format-fidelity behavior remains unchanged.

The measured eligible-sRGB workload and its observed improvement do not establish the memory behavior of those unsupported-format fallback paths. Physical capture formats have not been verified, so this evidence does not imply that every real Mac screenshot is eligible or benefits. No new fallback-format benchmark or delivery gate is introduced; this is the boundary of the existing measurements. Native final storage, reference effects, resource limits and fail-closed reported-effect-failure behavior remain. Required snapshot/patch/recursive failures refuse final output; non-nil GPU correctness and safe on-screen concealment are not guaranteed. Blur/pixelation stay cosmetic; opaque redaction plus checking the actual flattened deliverable remains necessary for sharing private content.

## Offline settings candidate 169: source scope, awaiting native acceptance

The pending 0.19 candidate adds a bounded local JSON configuration file and a draft-only annotation order editor. The schema is versioned, capped at 64 KiB, and rejects unknown or duplicate fields, invalid values and incomplete/duplicate tool permutations. It includes appearance, screenshot options, pin behavior, history limits, six named shortcuts and optional annotation order. It excludes capture/OCR/pin/history content, credentials, machine-local paths and display identities, save naming/automatic-write intent, and recording input-visualization opt-ins. It provides no cloud synchronization.

Import first presents saved-versus-incoming differences. Cancel preserves both saved preferences and the Settings draft. Apply verifies the review baseline and exact persistent key presence, probes changed shortcuts while normal registrations are suspended, and commits a synchronous logical UserDefaults transaction. An ordinary write failure restores touched values and absent keys; a separately observed external replacement is preserved and reported as an incomplete rollback. This is not crash-durable or cross-process atomic storage, nor a reservation against another application later claiming a shortcut. A history-limit change explicitly warns that subsequent pruning cannot be undone by restoring configuration.

The annotation setting orders 14 primary families while keeping every existing subtool and output action reachable. Reset and movement are draft-only until Save; malformed local order falls back without rewriting preferences. Native tests cover narrow and negative-origin capture/pin geometry, actual hit targets, preserved annotation documents, stale callbacks and output guard behavior.

The authored inventory adds 48 methods in five classes to all 1,897 accepted methods: 1,945 methods / 218 classes, unchanged four exhaustive processes and 420-second per-process limits. The focused selection becomes 1,413 methods. The source regression proves the original accepted inventory digest after removing only the declared additions. ### Candidate175 measured event-clock conversion

[Build175](https://github.com/dandibbert/picshot/actions/runs/38029115370), source `2577d82d7f79d5a8c227cb48c334d6dee876771e`, confirms that ARM's early table event is copied with only its Double timestamp changed: expected 2838.5901672083337 seconds, dequeued 2838.5901672079999, difference −3.3378455555066466e−10 seconds. Type 1, window 162, location [473.5,417], event number 1, click count 1 and modifier flags 0 all match. The window is key; the rejected event is restored before dispatch. Its raw report fails after 0.071605 seconds. This explains this equality rejection, but does not establish table selection, draft cancellation or final installer acceptance.

The follow-on compares the required native integer Quartz clock exactly, with no floating-point tolerance. Apple defines [CGEventTimestamp](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventtimestamp) as UInt64 nanoseconds since startup; [NSEvent.cgEvent](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsevent/cgevent) is optional. A missing or zero supplied clock fails before queueing, a missing or different dequeued clock is rejected and restored. Exact type/window/location guards remain and event number, click count and modifier equality are also required. Both native clocks, Double timestamps and other identity fields remain in positive and failed evidence; checker tampering rejects a one-nanosecond difference, missing clocks and unrelated event fields. This is an intentional native representation correction to the fixture, with all native test IDs, selection/draft/Cancel assertions and time bounds retained; native dispatch is still pending.
